Um... I wouldn't say it's like, a super strong feeling. It's definitely not like a deep sadness or anything like that. I don't want to sound like I'm falling apart over it, because I'm really not!

Um... I mean, I wouldn't say I do it like, obsessively or anything. I don't want you to think I'm just standing in front of a mirror all day instead of working. I'm a really good employee, I promise! 

But I guess... well, on my breaks, I do usually end up going to the bathroom to just... check on it. Just to make sure my makeup hasn't worn off or anything. And sometimes I'll try to pull my hair around my face a little bit to sort of camouflage it, if it's looking bad.

Um... I don't know if I'd use the word excessive, exactly. I really don't want to sound, like, overly obsessed or crazy or anything. But I guess... well, the main reason I came in is just because I keep getting stuck on this tiny uneven spot right around my nose and cheek.

Um... I really hope this doesn't sound too bad, I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around staring at myself all day! I try not to spend a crazy amount of time on it.

Um... gosh, I'm not exactly sure about the total minutes. I've never really timed it or anything. I promise it's not like hours and hours! 

I guess... maybe it's just a few minutes here and there? Um... yeah, I guess you could call it a preoccupation. I mean, I really try not to let it take over my mind, but... like I mentioned before, I just keep getting stuck on that tiny uneven spot around my nose and cheek.

Is it normal to have passing thoughts like that, or... or does that sound like I'm letting it distract me too much?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain social situations, interactions, or activities because you’re worried about how that spot on your face might look to others?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try not to let it stop me from doing things. I definitely don't want to be the kind of person who just hides away, you know? I want to be outgoing and social. \n\nI guess... sometimes if a friend wants to go somewhere with really bright, overhead lighting, like certain restaurants or... or taking a lot of close-up group selfies, I might suggest we do something else instead? But I still go out with them! I just try to steer us toward places where I feel a little more... presentable, I guess. \n\nI don't think I'm avoiding things completely, just... managing it? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm being a bad friend or too high maintenance. Is it okay to just prefer certain settings like that?\n\nDoctor: When you think about that spot on your face, does it bring up strong feelings like anxiety, shame, or sadness, or do you find it more of a mild annoyance that passes quickly?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say it's like, a super strong feeling. It's definitely not like a deep sadness or anything like that. I don't want to sound like I'm falling apart over it, because I'm really not! \n\nI guess it's more like... a mild anxiety? Just a little bit of worry, mostly about if other people are noticing it and thinking I don't take care of myself. Sometimes I feel a tiny bit embarrassed if I catch my reflection and it looks worse than I thought, but it usually passes pretty quick once I fix my makeup or adjust how I'm standing. \n\nIt's just a little annoyance, really. I try to just shake it off and keep smiling, especially at work. I mean, everybody has things they don't love about themselves, right? It's pretty normal to feel just a little bit self-conscious sometimes... isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over, like checking mirrors, fixing your makeup, or trying to cover up that spot, because it bothers you how it looks?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I wouldn't say I do it like, obsessively or anything. I don't want you to think I'm just standing in front of a mirror all day instead of working. I'm a really good employee, I promise! \n\nBut I guess... well, on my breaks, I do usually end up going to the bathroom to just... check on it. Just to make sure my makeup hasn't worn off or anything. And sometimes I'll try to pull my hair around my face a little bit to sort of camouflage it, if it's looking bad. \n\nAnd... I guess I do kind of look at the other girls who come through my line, the customers, and just sort of notice how smooth their skin is compared to mine. It's just a habit, really! But sometimes I guess I do get a little caught up in fixing it in the bathroom, and... well, I might have been a minute or two late getting back to the register a couple of times. \n\nBut I always apologize and work extra hard to make up for it! It's not like a huge problem or anything. That's just normal, right? To want to touch up your makeup on your break so you look nice for the customers?\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about the spot on your face ever caused you significant distress or made it hard for you to function in any part of your life, like at work, socially, or emotionally?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say it's like, ruined my life or anything drastic like that. I really try my best to keep it together and be a good worker. I'm actually hoping to get promoted to shift lead soon! \n\nBut... well, I guess it has caused a tiny bit of an issue at work lately. Like I mentioned before, I've been a little late coming back from my breaks because I get kind of stuck trying to fix my makeup in the bathroom... and my manager actually pulled me aside the other day to mention it. \n\nHe also said I've been moving a little slower at the register. I think it's just because I'm so focused on trying to keep my head angled away from the customers so they don't stare at my spot, you know? It just takes a little more concentration. \n\nI'm just really worried that it's making me look unreliable to him, right when I'm trying so hard to prove I deserve that promotion. I really don't want him to think I'm a bad employee! I'm trying to fix it, I promise. It's not like a major crisis, just a little bump in the road... right? I'm sure I can get it under control.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your concern about the spot on your face has ever caused you significant distress or made it hard for you to function in any part of your life, like at work, socially, or emotionally?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I think we actually just talked about that, right? About how it's been affecting my work a little bit? \n\nI really hope I explained it okay before. Like I said, it's just that my manager noticed I was taking a little too long on my breaks and moving a bit slower at the register. I don't think it's causing me, like, *significant* distress or anything. I mean, I still go to work every day, and I still hang out with my friends sometimes. I haven't stopped living my life! \n\nI just... I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and that my manager doesn't think less of me. I really am trying my best to just push through it and not let it get in the way. That makes sense, doesn't it? I mean, I answered that the right way, didn't I?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about the spot on your face causes you noticeable distress or impairment in your daily functioning, such as at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Um... I feel like we keep circling back to this, and I'm really sorry if I'm not giving you the answer you're looking for! I promise I'm trying to explain it right. \n\nI guess... well, like I mentioned, it is causing a little bit of a hiccup at work. Just because my manager noticed I was coming back from my breaks a few minutes late, and that I'm moving a little slower at the register when I'm trying to hide my face. \n\nIt's just... it's really stressful because I don't want him to think I'm unreliable, especially since I've been working so hard for that shift lead promotion. I really care about my job and I want to be seen as a good employee. But other than that, it's not like it's ruining my relationships or making me unable to function or anything. I'm still doing okay overall! Is that... is that what you were asking? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about the spot on your face feels excessive, or that it involves worrying about something that most people wouldn’t think is a big deal?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I'd use the word excessive, exactly. I really don't want to sound, like, overly obsessed or crazy or anything. Just to make sure my makeup hasn't worn off or anything. And sometimes I'll try to pull my hair around my face a little bit to sort of camouflage it, if it's looking bad. \n\nAnd... I guess I do kind of look at the other girls who come through my line, the customers, and just sort of notice how smooth their skin is compared to mine. It's just a habit, really! But sometimes I guess I do get a little caught up in fixing it in the bathroom, and... well, I might have been a minute or two late getting back to the register a couple of times. \n\nBut I always apologize and work extra hard to make up for it! It's not like a huge problem or anything. That's just normal, right? To want to touch up your makeup on your break so you look nice for the customers?\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about the spot on your face ever caused you significant distress or made it hard for you to function in any part of your life, like at work, socially, or emotionally?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say it's like, ruined my life or anything drastic like that. I really try my best to keep it together and be a good worker. I'm actually hoping to get promoted to shift lead soon! \n\nBut... well, I guess it has caused a tiny bit of an issue at work lately. Like I mentioned before, I've been a little late coming back from my breaks because I get kind of stuck trying to fix my makeup in the bathroom... and my manager actually pulled me aside the other day to mention it. \n\nHe also said I've been moving a little slower at the register. I think it's just because I'm so focused on trying to keep my head angled away from the customers so they don't stare at my spot, you know? It just takes a little more concentration. \n\nI'm just really worried that it's making me look unreliable to him, right when I'm trying so hard to prove I deserve that promotion. I really don't want him to think I'm a bad employee! I'm trying to fix it, I promise. It's not like a major crisis, just a little bump in the road... right? I'm sure I can get it under control.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your concern about the spot on your face has ever caused you significant distress or made it hard for you to function in any part of your life, like at work, socially, or emotionally?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I think we actually just talked about that, right? About how it's been affecting my work a little bit? \n\nI really hope I explained it okay before. Like I said, it's just that my manager noticed I was taking a little too long on my breaks and moving a bit slower at the register. I don't think it's causing me, like, *significant* distress or anything. I mean, I still go to work every day, and I still hang out with my friends sometimes. I haven't stopped living my life! \n\nI just... I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job and that my manager doesn't think less of me. I really am trying my best to just push through it and not let it get in the way. That makes sense, doesn't it? I mean, I answered that the right way, didn't I?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about the spot on your face causes you noticeable distress or impairment in your daily functioning, such as at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Um... I feel like we keep circling back to this, and I'm really sorry if I'm not giving you the answer you're looking for! I promise I'm trying to explain it right. \n\nI guess... well, like I mentioned, it is causing a little bit of a hiccup at work. Just because my manager noticed I was coming back from my breaks a few minutes late, and that I'm moving a little slower at the register when I'm trying to hide my face. \n\nIt's just... it's really stressful because I don't want him to think I'm unreliable, especially since I've been working so hard for that shift lead promotion. I really care about my job and I want to be seen as a good employee. But other than that, it's not like it's ruining my relationships or making me unable to function or anything. I'm still doing okay overall! Is that... is that what you were asking? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about the spot on your face feels excessive, or that it involves worrying about something that most people wouldn’t think is a big deal?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I'd use the word excessive, exactly. I really don't want to sound, like, overly obsessed or crazy or anything. But I guess... well, the main reason I came in is just because I keep getting stuck on this tiny uneven spot right around my nose and cheek. \n\nIt really stands out to me in photos, and especially under those harsh bright store lights at work. And the thing is... when I ask other people about it, they always say they don't even see it. So I guess you're right that most people don't think it's a big deal. \n\nBut even when they tell me that, it just always feels like it's the very first thing the customers and my managers notice when they look at me. I know it probably sounds a little silly, and I really try not to make a big fuss about it to anyone. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not coming across as too vain or anything... I'm just trying to answer your question the right way.\n\nDoctor: How much time do you spend each day on behaviors related to your concern about the spot on your face, like checking mirrors, adjusting makeup, or comparing yourself to others?\n\nPatient: Um... I really hope this doesn't sound too bad, I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around staring at myself all day! I try not to spend a crazy amount of time on it. \n\nBut... I guess this kind of ties into what I was saying earlier about my manager. When I'm on my breaks, I usually go to the bathroom, and I just end up checking my face in the mirror for a while. I try to fix my hair around it to sort of cover the spot up and make it less noticeable. And... well, when I'm working, I do catch myself comparing myself to the girls who come through my line, just checking to see if their skin is perfectly even. \n\nIt's mostly just the mirror checking on my breaks that takes up the actual time, though. Sometimes I just get so focused on trying to fix it that I take way too long, and that's why I end up being late getting back to the register. I'm really trying to be better about it so it doesn't affect my job! Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm not sounding too obsessive.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how many minutes total you spend each day specifically checking your face in the mirror or adjusting your makeup because of the spot?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I'm not exactly sure about the total minutes. I've never really timed it or anything. I promise it's not like hours and hours! \n\nI guess... maybe it's just a few minutes here and there? Like, when I'm on my fifteen-minute break, I might spend, I don't know, maybe five or ten minutes of it in the bathroom just quickly checking and touching up my makeup. And then maybe just a quick glance before my shift starts or when I get home. \n\nSo maybe... I don't know, twenty or thirty minutes total for the whole day? I really try to keep it quick, I don't want to be one of those girls who's just constantly staring in the mirror. I hope that doesn't sound like too much time... is that a normal amount of time to spend getting ready?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concern about the spot on your face feels like a preoccupation that's hard to control, even when you know it might not make sense?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess you could call it a preoccupation. I just don't want you to think I'm totally out of touch with reality or anything.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the spot on your face ever stopped you from doing something important at work or in your personal life, or made it hard for you to do something you normally would do?\n\nPatient: Um...
